[[İzmir]] 2) Meral Danış Beştaş [[Adana]] [[Adıyaman]] [[Pain]] [[Pain]] 6) The Leader of the Secret [[Ankara]] 7) Ayşe Acar Başaran [[Batman]] 8) Mehmet Ali Aslan [[Batman]] [[Batman]] [[Bingöl]] [[Bitlis]] [[Diyarbakır]] [[Diyarbakır]] [[Diyarbakır]] [[Diyarbakır]] [[Diyarbakır]] [[Diyarbakır]] [[Diyarbakır]] [[Diyarbakır]] [[Gaziantep]] [[Hakkari]] 22) Mehmet Emin Adıyaman [[Lug]] [[İstanbul]] From the mountains of the southern mountain ranges of Sivas, the Played Tea comes from the Sun's Tea, which emerges from the Snake Mountains, and the Sincan Tea from the Georgian Sea, which originated in the region of Gürlevik and Karabel, formed in the Curek Straits near Divrik, where it is called Played. The Played Tea is also the source of the Keban Dam. He follows the railway road of Sivas-Erzincan, which continues to flow in front of them, and joins the dam waters in the borders of the Keban Dam. The Sound Tea is fed by many large and small streams, and its length is 180km in the province of Sivas. Waters, streams, and teas have the ability to cleanse pollution to a certain extent. When that line is crossed, the water becomes extremely polluted and corrupt. The pollutants that cause pollution include physical or sensory changes, such as chemicals, pathogens, temperature change, and other types. Again, at times the excess increase in minerals such as calcium, sodium, iron, manganese is also viewed as a cause of pollution because of waste. The waste used in factories is usually toxic waste. Pathogens also cause water - carried diseases on humans and animals. The causes of changes in the physical chemistry of water include acidity (ph changes), electrical conductivity, temperature and algae buildup. The contamination of Playtea because of sewage and factory waste has a negative effect on vegetation and natural life. In particular, factory waste found in the region destroys natural life, destroying mountain goats, birds, and fish. The sewage waste from about 16 villages is also poured into the Stealth Tea. From time to time, the wastes of the iron steel mines found in the Divrik have been dyed red. As long as sewers and factory waste continue to be thrown into Tea, many plants and living species in the region will be destroyed within a few years. In light of all this information, it is imperative that a parliamentary study be opened to protect and take necessary measures to prevent contamination that threatens natural life and public health. Turkey to the chairman of the Grand National Assembly In order to investigate the violations of human rights, which have arisen due to the unlawful detention and deportation of refugees in Turkey, we propose and demand that Parliament be opened under Articles 98, 104, and 105. [[Van]] [[Diyarbakır]] 3) Filiz Kerestecioğlu Demir [[İstanbul]] [[İstanbul]] [[İstanbul]] [[İzmir]] [[Mardin]] [[Mardin]] [[Mardin]] [[Mwah.]] [[Mwah.]] [[Siirt]] [[Siirt]] [[Şırnak]] [[Şırnak]] [[Şırnak]] [[Şırnak]] [[Şanlıurfa]] [[Şanlıurfa]] [[Şanlıurfa]] [[Tunceli]] [[Van]] 23) Tuğba Hezer Özturk [[Van]] In December 2015, Amnesty International issued a report which provided strong evidence that Turkish authorities had captured and detained refugees and forced them to return to the war zones. The report pointed to a less-known human rights crisis in Turkey that has a negative impact on the lives of refugees and asylum seekers fleeing war and persecution in countries such as Iraq and Syria. This report was documenting the difficult situation among hundreds of refugees and asylum seekers who were captured in the land or sea borders of Turkey, who were long held, were not allowed to contact the outside world, and in some cases were forced back to their country in violation of Turkish law and international law. According to information from the detained refugees and asylum seekers to Amnesty International, it takes them days to reach the centre where they are finally detained because they are held in more than one location along the road and the detention centers in south and east of Turkey are more than 1,000km away from the west coast where they are captured. A 40-year-old Syrian refugee/dweller at Erzurum Refuge Center describes a period between late September and late November, where he was locked up in a room for seven days with his hands and feet tied. This person says to Amnesty International, "When they chain up your hands and feet, you feel like a slave, like you're not human." he said.
